Or they could lose everything. And, I mean, part of me knows it’s irrational, but it hits me so hard. It feels like I’m personally responsible, and I just... I can’t just move on from it. It's really taking a toll.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly checking or re-checking your work to try and relieve these feelings of responsibility and worry?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Constantly, actually. I'll reread deployment plans, audit logs... even old messages, just over and over. And it's not just the IT systems, I do it with the door locks at the office, too. Sometimes I find myself counting through the steps of a process in my head until it finally feels... safe, I guess. \n\nI've actually ended up staying at the office for hours after everyone else has gone home just doing this. If I try to force myself to leave or stop checking before it feels complete, I just... I start to panic. So I stay and keep checking.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these repetitive thoughts or urges to check things in areas of your life outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's... it's pretty much just at work. I mean, at home, I lock the front door and I don't think twice about it. I don't check the stove or worry about things like that in my personal life. It's really just tied to my job and the systems there, and I guess the physical office itself. But outside of that, no, I don't have those same urges.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you are prevented from checking or rechecking your work or the office environment?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned, it turns into full-blown panic. If I'm interrupted, or if, say, building security tells me I absolutely have to leave before I'm done... it's just this overwhelming sense of dread. \n\nMy heart starts pounding, and I get really agitated. It's like my mind is just screaming that something catastrophic is going to happen because I didn't finish making sure it was safe. I basically can't focus on a single other thing for the rest of the night. It just consumes me until I can get back and finish checking.\n\nDoctor: Has your need to check and recheck things at work affected your relationships with colleagues or friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely has. It's... it's getting bad at work. I actually missed a major cloud migration deadline recently because I just couldn't bring myself to finish signing off on the decisions. I had to keep going back over everything. Now, my team has basically started working around me. They don't rely on me the way they used to, which is tough to see. \n\nAnd outside of work... yeah, it's affecting my friends too. I used to go to basketball and salsa nights pretty regularly, but I've just stopped showing up. I'm either stuck at the office checking things, or I'm too exhausted from it all. It sucks because I'm losing those routines and the people that used to keep me grounded, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had recurring, intrusive thoughts or urges that aren't related to your job or work environment?\n\nPatient: No, um... not really. Like I was saying earlier, it really is just focused on my job. I don't get those kinds of thoughts about anything else. If I'm not dealing with the office or our systems, my brain doesn't... it doesn't get stuck on things like that. So, no, nothing outside of work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because... I just keep getting these thoughts. I work in IT, managing systems, and I keep having this overwhelming feeling that if I miss one setting, or one access rule, or maybe a warning sign... someone could get hurt. Or they could lose everything. And, I mean, part of me knows it’s irrational, but it hits me so hard. It feels like I’m personally responsible, and I just... I can’t just move on from it. It's really taking a toll.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly checking or re-checking your work to try and relieve these feelings of responsibility and worry?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Constantly, actually. I'll reread deployment plans, audit logs... even old messages, just over and over. And it's not just the IT systems, I do it with the door locks at the office, too. Sometimes I find myself counting through the steps of a process in my head until it finally feels... safe, I guess. \n\nI've actually ended up staying at the office for hours after everyone else has gone home just doing this. If I try to force myself to leave or stop checking before it feels complete, I just... I start to panic. So I stay and keep checking.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these repetitive thoughts or urges to check things in areas of your life outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's... it's pretty much just at work. I mean, at home, I lock the front door and I don't think twice about it. I don't check the stove or worry about things like that in my personal life. It's really just tied to my job and the systems there, and I guess the physical office itself. But outside of that, no, I don't have those same urges.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you are prevented from checking or rechecking your work or the office environment?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned, it turns into full-blown panic. If I'm interrupted, or if, say, building security tells me I absolutely have to leave before I'm done... it's just this overwhelming sense of dread. \n\nMy heart starts pounding, and I get really agitated. It's like my mind is just screaming that something catastrophic is going to happen because I didn't finish making sure it was safe. I basically can't focus on a single other thing for the rest of the night. It just consumes me until I can get back and finish checking.\n\nDoctor: Has your need to check and recheck things at work affected your relationships with colleagues or friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely has. It's... it's getting bad at work. I actually missed a major cloud migration deadline recently because I just couldn't bring myself to finish signing off on the decisions. I had to keep going back over everything. Now, my team has basically started working around me. They don't rely on me the way they used to, which is tough to see. \n\nAnd outside of work... yeah, it's affecting my friends too. I used to go to basketball and salsa nights pretty regularly, but I've just stopped showing up. I'm either stuck at the office checking things, or I'm too exhausted from it all. It sucks because I'm losing those routines and the people that used to keep me grounded, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had recurring, intrusive thoughts or urges that aren't related to your job or work environment?\n\nPatient: No, um... not really. Like I was saying earlier, it really is just focused on my job. I don't get those kinds of thoughts about anything else. If I'm not dealing with the office or our systems, my brain doesn't... it doesn't get stuck on things like that. So, no, nothing outside of work.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling like your overall quality of life is suffering because of these thoughts and behaviors?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely is. I mean, like I mentioned a minute ago, it's really taking a toll. I missed that huge cloud migration deadline because I just couldn't get through signing off on the decisions without checking everything over and over. And now my team is just... they're working around me. They don't rely on me anymore. \n\nAnd then my personal life is taking a hit too. I used to really look forward to basketball and salsa nights, but I've just stopped going. I'm losing the people and the routines that used to keep me grounded. So, yeah, my quality of life is definitely suffering. It's just... it's all slipping away.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other areas of your life, besides work and social relationships, where you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are causing problems?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's... it's just work and my social life, like I said. At home, or with other things, I don't really have these issues. It's really just contained to my job and how that spills over into me not having the energy or time for my friends anymore. So, no, nothing else comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any difficulty with your daily routines or activities at home due to these thoughts and behaviors?\n\nPatient: No, um, not really. It's... it's just work and my social life, like I said. At home, or with other things, I don't really have these issues. It's really just contained to my job and how that spills over into me not having the energy or time for my friends anymore. So, no, nothing else comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any difficulty with your daily routines or activities at home due to these thoughts and behaviors?\n\nPatient: No, um, not really. Like I was just saying, it doesn't really affect my stuff at home. When I'm just doing my normal daily routines around the house—you know, making dinner, doing chores, things like that—my brain doesn't get stuck like it does with the work systems. It's really just the job that triggers it. So my routines at home are fine.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed or distressed by these thoughts and behaviors most of the time?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say *most* of the time, no. Like I was just saying, it's really tied specifically to my job. \n\nWhen I'm working, or, you know, trying to sign off on those system migrations, then yes, absolutely, I feel incredibly overwhelmed. It's very distressing in those moments. But when I'm just at home, or away from the work systems, I feel fine. My mind is quiet. So... it's not a constant, all-day kind of distress. It just hits really hard when it comes to work.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these overwhelming feelings and urges to check things at work for more than a few months?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure, to be honest. I haven't... I haven't kept track of exactly how long it's been happening. It just sort of became my normal routine at work over time, so I couldn't tell you for certain if it's been more than a few months or not.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Constantly, actually. I'll reread deployment plans, audit logs... even old messages, just over and over. And it's not just the IT systems, I do it with the door locks at the office, too. Sometimes I find myself counting through the steps of a process in my head until it finally feels... safe, I guess. \n\nI've actually ended up staying at the office for hours after everyone else has gone home just doing this. If I try to force myself to leave or stop checking before it feels complete, I just... I start to panic. It's... it's pretty much just at work. I mean, at home, I lock the front door and I don't think twice about it. I don't check the stove or worry about things like that in my personal life. It's like my mind is just screaming that something catastrophic is going to happen because I didn't finish making sure it was safe. I basically can't focus on a single other thing for the rest of the night. Sometimes I find myself counting through the steps of a process in my head until it finally feels... safe, I guess. \n\nI've actually ended up staying at the office for hours after everyone else has gone home just doing this. If I try to force myself to leave or stop checking before it feels complete, I just... I start to panic. It's... it's pretty much just at work. I mean, at home, I lock the front door and I don't think twice about it. I don't check the stove or worry about things like that in my personal life. It's like my mind is just screaming that something catastrophic is going to happen because I didn't finish making sure it was safe. I basically can't focus on a single other thing for the rest of the night. It just consumes me until I can get back and finish checking.\n\nDoctor: Has your need to check and recheck things at work affected your relationships with colleagues or friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely has.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don't know if I avoid starting tasks... I mean, I show up and try to do my job. But I definitely avoid finishing them. 

Like I mentioned with the cloud migration, I'll put off making the final decisions. I'll do the work, but when it's time to actually sign off on it, I... I just can't. So I guess I avoid the parts of the job that require a final approval, because I know I'll just get stuck second-guessing everything. But it's not like I'm intentionally hiding from new assignments, if that makes sense.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, like I said earlier, it's definitely causing major problems for me. Most days... yeah, it's a struggle. At work, missing that cloud migration deadline was a huge blow. It's... it's getting bad at work. I actually missed a major cloud migration deadline recently because I just couldn't bring myself to finish signing off on the decisions. I had to keep going back over everything. Now, my team has basically started working around me. They don't rely on me the way they used to, which is tough to see. \n\nAnd outside of work... yeah, it's affecting my friends too. I haven't been keeping track of the exact time. It's been a while, but I can't give you a specific number of months.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you been experiencing these thoughts and behaviors consistently over the past 6 months or longer?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 43 / computer_or_information_systems_manager / graduate\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Plain patient.\nA plain patient describes their symptoms and experiences in a direct and straightforward manner, providing clear and relevant answers to the doctor's questions.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Let me think... I mean, honestly, no. I can't really remember a specific time when my mind wasn't doing this. Maybe when I was a lot younger, but... I don't know. I work in IT, managing systems, and I keep having this overwhelming feeling that if I miss one setting, or one access rule, or maybe a warning sign... someone could get hurt. It's really taking a toll.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly checking or re-checking your work to try and relieve these feelings of responsibility and worry?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Constantly, actually. I'll reread deployment plans, audit logs... even old messages, just over and over. And it's not just the IT systems, I do it with the door locks at the office, too. Sometimes I find myself counting through the steps of a process in my head until it finally feels... safe, I guess. \n\nI've actually ended up staying at the office for hours after everyone else has gone home just doing this. If I try to force myself to leave or stop checking before it feels complete, I just... I start to panic. So I stay and keep checking.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience these repetitive thoughts or urges to check things in areas of your life outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's... it's pretty much just at work. I mean, at home, I lock the front door and I don't think twice about it. I don't check the stove or worry about things like that in my personal life. But outside of that, no, I don't have those same urges.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you are prevented from checking or rechecking your work or the office environment?\n\nPatient: Well, like I mentioned, it turns into full-blown panic. It's like my mind is just screaming that something catastrophic is going to happen because I didn't finish making sure it was safe. I basically can't focus on a single other thing for the rest of the night. It just consumes me until I can get back and finish checking.\n\nDoctor: Has your need to check and recheck things at work affected your relationships with colleagues or friends?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it definitely has. It's... it's getting bad at work. I actually missed a major cloud migration deadline recently because I just couldn't bring myself to finish signing off on the decisions. I had to keep going back over everything. Now, my team has basically started working around me. They don't rely on me the way they used to, which is tough to see. \n\nAnd outside of work... yeah, it's affecting my friends too. I used to go to basketball and salsa nights pretty regularly, but I've just stopped showing up. I'm either stuck at the office checking things, or I'm too exhausted from it all. It sucks because I'm losing those routines and the people that used to keep me grounded, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had recurring, intrusive thoughts or urges that aren't related to your job or work environment?\n\nPatient: No, um... not really. And now my team is just... they're working around me. They don't rely on me anymore. \n\nAnd then my personal life is taking a hit too. I used to really look forward to basketball and salsa nights, but I've just stopped going. I'm losing the people and the routines that used to keep me grounded. So, yeah, my quality of life is definitely suffering. It's just... it's all slipping away.\n\nDoctor: Are there any other areas of your life, besides work and social relationships, where you feel like these thoughts and behaviors are causing problems?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's... it's just work and my social life, like I said. At home, or with other things, I don't really have these issues. It's really just contained to my job and how that spills over into me not having the energy or time for my friends anymore. So, no, nothing else comes to mind.\n\nDoctor: Are you experiencing any difficulty with your daily routines or activities at home due to these thoughts and behaviors?\n\nPatient: No, um, not really. Like I was just saying, it doesn't really affect my stuff at home. When I'm just doing my normal daily routines around the house—you know, making dinner, doing chores, things like that—my brain doesn't get stuck like it does with the work systems. It's really just the job that triggers it. So my routines at home are fine.\n\nDoctor: Are you feeling overwhelmed or distressed by these thoughts and behaviors most of the time?\n\nPatient: Um, I wouldn't say *most* of the time, no. Like I was just saying, it's really tied specifically to my job. \n\nWhen I'm working, or, you know, trying to sign off on those system migrations, then yes, absolutely, I feel incredibly overwhelmed. It's very distressing in those moments. But when I'm just at home, or away from the work systems, I feel fine. My mind is quiet. So... it's not a constant, all-day kind of distress. It just hits really hard when it comes to work.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these overwhelming feelings and urges to check things at work for more than a few months?\n\nPatient: I'm really not sure, to be honest. I haven't... I haven't kept track of exactly how long it's been happening. It just sort of became my normal routine at work over time, so I couldn't tell you for certain if it's been more than a few months or not.\n\nDoctor: Can you try to estimate how long you've been experiencing these overwhelming feelings and urges to check things at work?\n\nPatient: I really wish I could give you a better timeline, but I... I just can't. It kind of all blurs together with the different projects at work. It crept up so gradually that I honestly don't have a good guess for you. I just know it's how things are now.\n\nDoctor: Have these feelings and urges to check things been causing you significant distress or problems at work or in your social life for more than 6 months?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I really can't speak to the six-month mark. I'm just losing the routines and the people that used to keep me grounded. I just haven't kept track of it like that. It might be, but I honestly can't give you a firm yes or no on that specific amount of time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain tasks or responsibilities at work because of these thoughts and behaviors?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I avoid starting tasks... I mean, I show up and try to do my job. But I definitely avoid finishing them. \n\nLike I mentioned with the cloud migration, I'll put off making the final decisions. I'll do the work, but when it's time to actually sign off on it, I... I just can't. So I guess I avoid the parts of the job that require a final approval, because I know I'll just get stuck second-guessing everything. But it's not like I'm intentionally hiding from new assignments, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms been causing you significant distress or impairment in your job or social life for most days over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yeah, like I said earlier, it's definitely causing major problems for me.
